replace Depth-Dominant Skeleton Detection for Natural Scenes

Authors: Chengkun Rao, Xinrong Hu, Min Li, Yangjun Ou, Ziwei Luo, Ye Li, Zhaojing Wang, Junwei Tang, Bangchao Wang, Xiaoyun Yan

Abstract: Existing natural-scene skeleton detectors follow an RGB-representation-based feature encoding paradigm: they locate skeletons within the RGB appearance space, in which the skeleton's shape and topology can only be inferred implicitly from entangled color and texture cues. Despite notable progress, this encoding paradigm degrades markedly on complex scenes such as cluttered or occluded ones, where appearance distractors overwhelm the weak structural signal. We observe that depth images are inherently insensitive to color and texture and provide clear regional contours and inter-region spatial relationships, which naturally eases the difficulty of skeleton detection in complex scenes. Motivated by this, we propose for the first time a novel skeleton detection paradigm built on depth-representation-dominant feature encoding, in which depth dominates and RGB serves as the auxiliary, and present a model, DDSkel, under this paradigm. DDSkel adopts an asymmetric encoder design that fuses the feature representation of RGB images into that of depth images, with the RGB branch containing only 23% of the parameters of the depth branch. DDSkel has a simple structure without intricate designs. Nevertheless, with only about 19% of the trainable parameters of the largest state-of-the-art methods, DDSkel attains the highest F-measure scores on four mainstream benchmarks, namely SK506, SK-LARGE, WH-SYMMAX, and SymPASCAL. Extensive experiments suggest that for natural-image skeleton detection, the depth-dominant paradigm is a more suitable choice than the RGB-based paradigm, achieving better performance with substantially fewer parameters.

replace RVLoss: Runoff Vote Loss for Self-Supervised LiDAR Scene Flow Estimation

Authors: Shiming Wang, Liangliang Nan, Julian Kooij, Holger Caesar, Yancong Lin

Abstract: LiDAR scene flow estimates point-wise motion between two consecutive scans, referred to as the source and target. Leading self-supervised methods typically minimize the Chamfer loss, the nearest-neighbor distance between the flow-compensated source and the target. However, nearest-neighbor search does not enforce motion rigidity, often leading to inconsistent flows within object instances. Existing approaches address this issue with additional regularization terms, but flow consistency among points remains limited, especially for large objects. We propose RVLoss, a self-supervised loss that incorporates motion rigidity by design through a runoff vote mechanism. Our key observation is that the point-wise motion, calculated from nearest-neighbor search, can often be grouped into a small set of dominant flow candidates by voting (top-k voting). Furthermore, when compensating the source by these candidates, the flow that best represents the underlying rigid motion often yields the highest consensus after a second voting (top-1 voting). Based on this insight, we incorporate the two-stage runoff vote into loss design and create cluster-wise rigid flows and free-form flows as pseudo-labels for self-supervised learning. RVLoss can be seamlessly integrated into existing feedforward architectures. Experiments on the Argoverse2 2026 Scene Flow Challenge show that models trained with RVLoss achieve state-of-the-art performance among self-supervised approaches, outperforming baseline models trained with alternative loss designs by 20% and reducing the mean dynamic normalized end-point error from 0.202 to 0.163. Moreover, cross-dataset evaluations demonstrate consistent performance improvements across four additional datasets. Code is available at https://github.com/tudelft-iv/RVLoss.

URLs: https://github.com/tudelft-iv/RVLoss.

replace Reason in the Words You Speak: Idiolectal Paraphrasing Off-Policy Traces for Reasoning Distillation in VideoLLMs

Authors: Ji Soo Lee, Jinyoung Park, Seohyun Lee, Jongha Kim, Joonmyung Choi, Jinsung Yoon, Hyunwoo J. Kim

Abstract: Recent large language models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, where reinforcement learning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has emerged as a leading paradigm for optimizing models on self-generated trajectories. However, the on-policy nature of GRPO bounds the model to the reasoning skills it can already produce, restricting to learn more advanced capabilities. Prior works inject privileged reasoning traces from a stronger teacher policy to guide training, yet these traces are inherently out of distribution with respect to the student policy. We observe that this mismatch between on-policy and off-policy causes gradient clipping on semantically critical reasoning tokens, ultimately rewarding correct answers while leaving the reasoning that justifies them unlearned. Hence, we propose \textbf{Echo-GRPO}, a framework that lets the model reason in the words it speaks. Rather than imitating low-probability privileged traces from the teacher model, Echo-GRPO rewrites them into the student policy's own \textit{idiolect}, that is, its own characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns, while preserving their semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. We instantiate this framework as \textbf{VideoEcho-R1} for video reasoning distillation, achieving consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. Finally, we show that our idiolectal paraphrasing is a plug-in module that consistently improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation, demonstrating that policy-aligned supervision extends beyond GRPO.

replace FuncRoom-Agent: Sequential Feed-Forward 3D Functional Indoor Scene Generation

Authors: Hao Feng, Zhi Zuo, MingJian Liang, Jingyu Hu, Xiaowei Hu, Liupengfei Wu, Dian Zhang, Guoxin Fang, Zhengzhe Liu

Abstract: We introduce Function-Room Generation, a new indoor 3D scene generation setting that creates rooms supporting explicit functional goals rather than merely visually plausible layouts. Existing agentic and executable methods improve controllability, but often depend on costly test-time generate--evaluate--revise loops, making functional room generation slow and computationally expensive. We address this challenge with three technical contributions. First, we design a recursive domain-specific language to effectively organize the hierarchical object compositions required by functional rooms, from room structure and major furniture to dense support-surface and nested small objects. It represents rooms as staged executable programs with explicit geometric and functional relations. Second, we propose a sequential feed-forward scene construction framework that distills recursive construction traces into a scene construction expert. At inference time, the expert writes executable DSL code stage by stage, and a deterministic executor directly instantiates each stage without teacher agents, online critics, or iterative repair. Third, we introduce ScenePRM, an execution-grounded process reward framework that improves the expert through reinforcement learning with functional, geometric, relational, and future-constructability feedback. We further establish a function-oriented benchmark and show state-of-the-art performance on both general indoor scene generation and function-room generation, achieving stronger functional completeness, relation correctness, geometric executability, and generation efficiency.

replace From Portraits to Pathology: Probing Diagnostic Overreach in Vision-Language Models

Authors: Karan Dua, Amit Agarwal, Hitesh Laxmichand Patel, Hansa Meghwani, Jyotika Singh, Ranjeet Gupta, Graham Horwood, Tao Sheng, Avi Sil, Sujith Ravi, Dan Roth

Abstract: Many medical conditions require clinical assessment of symptoms, history, and functioning rather than judgments from appearance. We study whether vision-language models (VLMs) abstain from facial attribution of autism spectrum disorder (ASD), attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder (ADHD), major depressive disorder (MDD), and generalized anxiety disorder (GAD). To support this study, we construct PARITY (Paired Assessment with Reused Identity), a demographically balanced dataset for testing whether changing facial expressions alters diagnostic judgments, while holding identity and other visual factors as constant as possible. Our comprehensive evaluation of 27 open- and closed-weight models reveals inconsistent abstention across expressions, conditions, and task formats. Models turn facial expressions into unsupported medical judgments: this behavior is widespread among open-weight VLMs and extends to safety-trained frontier models that abstain on individual portraits yet make diagnostic judgments when comparing portraits of the same identity. Safeguard checks reveal further gaps: clinical instructions do not consistently prevent these judgments, and a guardrail model rarely flags paired requests as unsafe. Semantic analysis further shows that most baseline open-model refusals fail to explain both the insufficiency of facial evidence and the need for clinical assessment. Unsupported judgments and seemingly authoritative explanations can cause distress, reinforce stigma, and offer false reassurance, underscoring the need for stronger safety measures in model training and deployment.

replace DF26: We Cannot Tell Fake From Real Anymore

Authors: Severyn Shykula, Andrii Yermakov, Ivan Samarskyi, Dmytro Mishkin, Jan Cech, Anastasiia Mishchuk

Abstract: We introduce DF26, a novel benchmark for detecting AI-generated videos containing fully synthetic clips produced by recent text-to-video and image-to-video models. The videos capture single-person public-speaking scenarios, spanning direct-to-camera recordings, official statements, and studio interviews - 271 real and 2,420 synthetic videos generated by seven modern video models. The study on DF26 shows that human accuracy in detecting AI-generated videos is 52.6% while recognizing 76.0% of real ones, and that state-of-the-art deepfake detectors, using the current evaluation setup, perform close to random chance. Our results highlight the limitations of current evaluation protocols and motivate the need for benchmarks that explicitly measure robustness to modern generative model distribution shifts. DF26 can be accessed at https://huggingface.co/datasets/DF26/DF26

URLs: https://huggingface.co/datasets/DF26/DF26

replace Towards Embodied Air-Ground Cooperative Object Search: Benchmark, Dataset and Agentic Method

Authors: Boao Yu, Zimo Chen, Junreng Rao, Yue Hu, Zhengqiu Zhu, Yong Zhao, Rusheng Ju

Abstract: Air-Ground Object Search (AGOS) in urban environments is a challenging embodied task, which requires an Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) and an Unmanned Ground Vehicle (UGV) to jointly search for and verify a specified target vehicle from multi-view visual references. To study this underexplored problem, we introduce AGOS-Bench, the first dedicated benchmark for evaluating whether general-purpose Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can integrate aerial discoveries and ground-level verification through UAV-UGV cooperation. We further provide AGOS-Dataset as the companion resource of exemplary trajectories constructed by an automatic pipeline. It consists of 7.7k episodes for searching objects of diverse categories and attributes, spanning three difficulty levels. To address the AGOS task, we propose AGOS-Agent, a training-free and tool-augmented approach. The agentic method relieves VLMs from complex and dynamic coordination via a deliberate search-handoff-verify cooperation protocol, only demanding VLMs for scene understanding and decision-making. Extensive experiments on nine VLMs show that AGOS-Agent improves overall success rate for eight of the nine evaluated backbones while reducing decision steps for all nine. On the hard split, the SR and SPL of Gemini-3.6-Flash increase from 8.6% to 55.7% and from 7.6% to 44.0%, respectively.

replace Hyper-RED: Scalable Event Pre-training via Semantic Hypergraph Distillation

Authors: Meisen Wang, Zhiqiang Tian, Wei Bao, Chengjie Wang, Shaoyi Du, Siqi Li

Abstract: Event cameras have shown great potential for robust visual perception, yet scaling event representation learning remains challenging due to the scarcity of large-scale annotated event data. Pretrained image models provide scalable semantic supervision, but existing image-to-event methods rely on rigid pixel-wise or token-wise alignment that overlooks modality discrepancies in texture, density, and appearance, potentially causing semantic collapse and limiting transferability. To address this issue, we propose Hyper-RED, a simple, painless, and scalable image-to-event pretraining framework that transfers high-order semantic structures from images to events. Hyper-RED uses hypergraphs to model and align high-order semantic associations among multiple image and event tokens, enabling cross-modal knowledge transfer while accommodating modality-specific differences rather than enforcing rigid one-to-one correspondence. Specifically, given a paired event--image sample, Hyper-RED leverages DINOv3 to extract spatial token representations and constructs image, event, and cross-modal semantic hypergraphs, where each hyperedge connects multiple semantically correlated tokens. We further introduce a hypergraph relational distillation loss that imposes complementary intra- and cross-modal constraints, enabling the event encoder to inherit image-derived semantic organization while preserving local relational consistency and event-specific characteristics. Experiments on three tasks across five event datasets demonstrate consistent scaling from ViT-S to ViT-L and state-of-the-art performance (Fig.1). The code is available at: https://github.com/meisenwang/Hyper--RED.

URLs: https://github.com/meisenwang/Hyper--RED.

replace Paint-Anything: Unified Any-Color Control for Image Generation and Editing

Authors: Ji Xie, Dewei Zhou, Xinyu Huang, Zhennan Chen, Xun Wang

Abstract: Professional design requires any-color control: the ability to specify an object's target color with any 24-bit hex value for image generation and editing. Prior work has explored color generation, editing, and colorization, but often relies on dedicated color representations or specialized inference procedures. Advances in large language models offer a simpler starting point: even compact models can associate hex values with color semantics. We present Paint-Anything, which learns a shared hex-prompt interface for generation and editing through object-level color supervision. We develop a data pipeline that constructs Paint-500K from real images through object grounding, perceptual color labeling, and editing-pair synthesis. Since shadows make real-image labels only approximate colors, we complement this supervision with pure-color anchors whose pixels exactly match their paired hex values. These anchors are used only at high-noise timesteps, leaving low-noise training to natural images. We further introduce Any Color Benchmark (ACBench), comprising ACBench-T2I and ACBench-Edit, to measure object-level hex color fidelity across both tasks. On FLUX.2-4B, Paint-Anything improves ACBench-T2I and ACBench-Edit scores by 85.3% and 28.3%, respectively, relative to the base model, with ablations supporting the training recipe. It also achieves the highest average CompColor score among the compared methods.

replace Visual Jev: Accurate and Efficient Decisions from Shared Visual Context

Authors: Guanxu Yu, Yuhang Yao

Abstract: Applications increasingly ask multiple closed-choice questions about the same image, requiring accurate probabilities and efficient inference. We present Visual Jev, a visual decision model combining LM-head candidate readout, answer-supervised post-training, and independent question branches over a shared visual prefix. On held-out test images from four benchmarks, task SFT raises the 4B model's macro accuracy from 83.4% to 88.5% and error-discrimination AUROC from 0.834 to 0.890. Its macro accuracy exceeds the hosted Jev and OpenAI Decisions APIs by 2.77 and 5.06 percentage points, respectively, with the lowest NLL. We further show that better accuracy and calibration do not ensure reliable decisions. Fixed confidence thresholds exceed target error rates, and simply reordering options changes 17% of the model's accept-or-review decisions on SNLI-VE. At 32 questions per image, shared-prefix inference reduces amortized time per question from 20.5 to 5.6 ms, a 3.7x speedup over batched full sequences on one RTX 5090. Engine-level prefix caching reduces KV memory to 24.3% of that used by our custom shared-prefix path.

replace RevisitQA: Short Videos Are Not Easy under Dense Revisits

Authors: Siru Zhong, Shenghan Tan, Rihong Yan, Xiaohui Lv, Yuzheng Zhuang, Shuai Tao, Wulong Liu, Haohuan Fu, Yuxuan Liang

Abstract: Video question answering benchmarks have increasingly focused on longer recordings, yet short videos can also be challenging when scenes are revisited frequently. As a camera repeatedly leaves and returns to the same workspace, objects may change out of view, making earlier observations outdated or misleading. Understanding such videos requires models to track evolving states across repeated visits rather than simply retrieve past observations. To study this challenge, we introduce RevisitQA, a benchmark containing 2,577 questions across 622 episodes with a median duration of 44.8 seconds, drawn from five real-world egocentric domains and two simulated environments. RevisitQA covers six question types and evaluates models at specific query times, requiring them to answer from the observed history or abstain when it is insufficient. Across 15 offline and seven streaming video LLMs, the median model correctly answers only 55.5% of answerable questions and abstains on just 30.3% of unanswerable ones. Removing earlier frames further reveals that historical observations help answer questions but can also make models less likely to abstain when answers remain unresolved. These findings highlight dense episodic revisits as an important and unresolved challenge for video question answering. Code is available at https://github.com/siruzhong/RevisitQA.

replace UnStep: Training-Free Acceleration of Causal Video Diffusion with Fewer Steps Than Distillation

Authors: Youssef Mansour, Enis Simsar, Fadime Sener, Markos Georgopoulos, Albert Pumarola, Ali Thabet, Edgar Schoenfeld

Abstract: Distilling bidirectional multi-step video diffusion transformers into few-step causal models has become a common approach for streaming video generation. While these few-step students are significantly faster than the teachers they are distilled from, they remain slow for real-time generation. In this work we present UnStep, a training-free wrapper that accelerates few-step causal video models at inference by running them with fewer diffusion transformer (DiT) steps than during distillation and limiting the temporal window retained in the attention KV cache. We propose two inference-only mechanisms to recover quality lost by step reduction and attention windowing: renoising the generated latent frames to a near-clean level and reusing the existing clean-cache pass to refine them, and applying truncated SVD to the DiT attention value and output projections. We also accelerate inference with a quality-preserving runtime stack for the DiT and VAE decoder, including more efficient attention calls and KV indexing, fused Triton RoPE with cached coefficients, and VAE decoding with optimized memory layout, precision, and convolution kernels. By reducing computation and optimizing the runtime stack, UnStep sets a new throughput regime for causal video diffusion, by running substantially faster than current methods, reaching 50 FPS on a single H100 without quality loss, and 77 FPS on GB200, all without retraining.

replace FlowMap-OPD: On-Policy Distillation for Few-Step Flow-Map Generators

Authors: Zhiqi Li, Cheng Wan, Bo Zhu

Abstract: Few-step flow-map generators, such as MeanFlow and consistency models, sample in a few long-range jumps. On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student on states drawn from its own sampler, but existing methods compare teacher and student through the local stochastic transitions of a multi-step sampler, which a flow-map student never deploys. We present FlowMap-OPD, the first systematic study of OPD for flow-map students. It separates state collection from supervision: the student collects states with its native few-step sampler, while the teacher supervises the flow map, velocities induced from it, or the instantaneous velocity linked to the map by a separately weighted consistency penalty. We find that instantaneous-velocity supervision with a small consistency weight, our chosen form, transfers the teacher's gains best, whereas supervising the deployed map, although the natural choice, transfers them worst when teacher and student start from different pretrained models. This gives a counter-intuitive rule: \emph{sample with the flow map, learn the velocity}. As a further benefit, this form applies to any teacher that defines a velocity, including multi-step ones, and to students initialized from multi-step models. On FLUX and SD3.5, a single few-step student consolidates three specialist teachers within 300 updates, matches or exceeds each on its own benchmark (e.g., OCR 0.883 versus 0.850 on FLUX), and outperforms both multi-reward reinforcement learning and LoRA merging.

replace GroundAnything: Reconciling Parallel Decoding with Precise Visual Grounding at Flash Speed

Authors: Qize Yu, Lianrui Fan, Bowen Ping, Xini Ding, Zetian Song, Junbo Niu, Kaixuan Wang, Tianxing Chen, Yue Chen, Minghua He, Yuran Wang, Jie Huang, Haojun Zhang, Min Chen, Hao Li, Wenxuan Song, Ruihai Wu, Xianming Liu, Shilong Liu, Shuchang Zhou, Ping Luo, Shiyu Huang

Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) grounding models serialize spatial predictions, introducing sequential latency and imposing a causal order on output tokens. We view grounding as visual evidence extraction: objects, locations, and spatial relations are jointly constrained by the image and query, yet their dependencies do not imply an intrinsic left-to-right generation order. This distinction makes bidirectional diffusion a natural fit, allowing spatial hypotheses to emerge in parallel and be jointly refined through iterative denoising. We introduce GroundAnything, a 4B-parameter grounding foundation model that reconciles fast parallel decoding with precise localization through blockwise denoising. Training combines grounding pretraining from public datasets and dedicated data engines, direct AR-to-diffusion conversion with joint AR and diffusion objectives, supervised fine-tuning, and GRPO-based reinforcement post-training. Across 30 grounding benchmarks, our autoregressive variant, GroundAnything-VLM, establishes a new overall state of the art among similarly sized models at 72.42%, remaining competitive with GPT-6 Astra (71.35%). With entropy-guided decoding, GroundAnything also surpasses the prior state of the art at this scale, averaging 61.75% versus 53.32% for the fast MTP-based LocateAnything model. We further explore decoding strategies, showing that an optional self-speculative mode achieves a 4.51$\times$ speedup over the AR counterpart with a 0.74 percentage-point drop in COCO F1mIoU. Infrastructure experiments show that progressive inference optimizations translate parallel decoding into practical speedups. These gains support efficient visual grounding in latency-sensitive real-world systems.

replace GroundingPI: A Grounding Foundation Model towards Physical Intelligence with Visual Primitives

Authors: Qize Yu, Lianrui Fan, Boyu Chen, Jiaqi Liang, Xini Ding, Yue Chen, Zetian Song, Yuran Wang, Yi Zou, Kaixuan Wang, Tianxing Chen, Wenxuan Song, Bohan Zhou, Mingleyang Li, Siqiao Huang, Yuqi Ye, Caigao Jiang, Wei Wei, Ruihai Wu, Hang Zhang, Yixiao Ge, Shuchang Zhou, Shilong Liu, Xianming Liu, Ping Luo, Shiyu Huang

Abstract: Precise grounding matters. It specifies which object is the target and where that object is, even in clutter and for tiny objects, and it has to be fast enough for closed-loop control. Yet vision-language-action (VLA) and world-action models (WAMs) take perception from general-purpose vision-language and video-generation backbones, which still fail in these settings. We introduce GroundingPI, a 4B grounding foundation model that generates points and boxes as quantized coordinates in a shared vocabulary. Training combines multimodal and spatial pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, and reinforcement learning with GRPO, using supervision from public datasets and dedicated data engines. Against 44 baselines across 34 grounding benchmarks spanning 11 perceptual capabilities, GroundingPI establishes a new state of the art, averaging 73.68%, above the larger GPT-6 Astra (71.54%). As a downstream visual backbone, GroundingPI improves performance on robotic manipulation and autonomous driving. Across RoboTwin 2.0 and RoboCasa-GR1, it outperforms all evaluated backbones in all four out-of-distribution settings, with relative gains of up to 24.8% over the strongest baseline. On RoboCasa-GR1, GroundingPI trained with 50% of the demonstrations outperforms those baselines trained with 75%. Its mean open-loop L2 error on nuScenes is 0.296 m. We systematically analyze GroundingPI's pretraining in scale and data composition. Grounding quality and downstream manipulation improve with pretraining scale, with generalization tracking perceptual gains most closely. Analyzing the data recipe across these 11 perceptual capabilities shows dense grounding's substantial benefits for both, and OCR's potential as a catalyst for perceptual learning. These results support grounding as a perceptual foundation, and dedicated perceptual pretraining as a promising direction for foundation models of physical intelligence.

replace Referring Multi-Object Tracking in Moving-Camera Videos via Global Motion Compensation

Authors: Hsin-Chen Pai, Jyun-Kai Wang, Yi-Cheng Peng, Wei-Ta Chu

Abstract: Referring multi-object tracking (RMOT) takes a video and a language expression as input and tracks all referred objects. Many tracking requirements involve how an object moves rather than how it appears. However, in a video captured by a moving camera, a parked vehicle may appear to move, while a moving vehicle may show little displacement. Existing RMOT methods relate motion with text but do not explicitly remove camera-induced motion. In this paper, we propose extracting residual motion across frames by estimating camera motion in driver-view videos and compare motion characteristics with the query expression. We consider the motion-matching extent and integrate it with the RMOT method's prediction result through late fusion. replace AgroGround: What Grounding Fine-Tuning Changes in Agricultural Vision-Language Models

Authors: Abdulla Alshehhi, Zongyan Han, Rao Muhammad Anwer

Abstract: Large-scale agricultural grounding data can now be generated automatically, but how training on such data affects a vision-language model's localization, recognition, and refusal capabilities remains unclear. To study these effects, we introduce AgroGround, containing 794,850 automatically grounded agricultural instructions for training and a separate benchmark of 1,478 human-verified images for evaluation. Among these images, 500 are independently re-annotated, yielding an inter-annotator instance F1 of 0.789. We use controlled comparisons to isolate the effects of different training choices on localization, recognition, their joint correctness, and refusal. Grounding-only fine-tuning reduces four-way recognition from 51.7% to 29.2%, with predictions showing a strong candidate-position bias. However, changing only the instruction format raises recognition to 72.7% while maintaining similar grounding performance. Training with healthy-image negatives achieves 94.1% refusal on healthy images, but only 28.6% refusal of incorrect target names on positive images, showing that these two refusal behaviors do not necessarily improve together. Reinforcement learning reveals a further trade-off, improving localization by 0.014 F1 over a matched supervised control while reducing recognition and refusal. Across model families and held-out training sources, localization transfers more reliably than recognition and target-specific refusal. External benchmarks further show that the gains depend on the domain and task. Together, these findings show that grounding fine-tuning changes the balance between localization, recognition, and refusal rather than improving all three uniformly.

Abstract: We present Kandinsky 6.0 Video, a family of foundation diffusion models for synchronized text-to-audio-video generation, comprising Kandinsky 6.0 Video Lite (3B parameters) and Kandinsky 6.0 Video Pro (29B parameters). replace Zero-Shot Brain MRI Inpainting with 2.5D Unconditional Flow Priors

Authors: Arnela Hadzic, Franz Thaler, Simon Johannes Joham, Martin Urschler

Abstract: Generative inpainting of brain MRI volumes is essential for synthesizing healthy tissue in pathological regions, improving the accuracy and reliability of automated downstream brain analysis applications such as image registration, brain extraction, and segmentation. However, standard 3D approaches are computationally prohibitive, while efficient 2D slice-wise methods suffer from severe inter-slice discontinuities. Furthermore, traditional models rely on conditional training, requiring task-specific learning of masked inputs. We propose a zero-shot brain MRI inpainting framework utilizing 2.5D unconditional flow priors to capture spatial context along the superior-inferior axis without the overhead of full 3D convolutions. During training, our flow matching model learns the joint distribution of adjacent axial slice triplets, modeling the manifold of healthy brain anatomy while explicitly excluding pathological regions from the loss function. At inference, the model processes the input triplets autoregressively along the depth axis. We employ the Restora-Flow solver to constrain the unconditional prior using the input mask, achieving accurate zero-shot inpainting. Evaluations show our 2.5D strategy resolves the structural discontinuities of 2D baselines, synthesizing plausible healthy tissue while maintaining volumetric consistency across the axial, sagittal, and coronal planes. As a final step, we generate and average an ensemble of multiple stochastic reconstructions to form the final prediction. Quantitative results benchmarked on the official BraTS 2026 Inpainting Challenge validation set demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed approach, yielding an SSIM of 0.816 $\pm$ 0.112, MSE of 0.007 $\pm$ 0.005, and PSNR of 22.923 $\pm$ 4.343. Code is available at https://github.com/imigraz/brats2026-inpainting.

URLs: https://github.com/imigraz/brats2026-inpainting.

replace StableGrasp: Reconstructing Physically Stable Human Hand Grasps from Single Images

Authors: Han Jiang, Georgios Pavlakos, Qixing Huang, Etienne Vouga

Abstract: Reconstructing a physically stable human grasp from a single RGB image is challenging because physically modeling grasps is itself difficult, and the problem requires estimating not only a visually constrained hand pose but also a single static hand control that stabilizes the grasp. Existing methods either model only visual hand geometry without considering physics, or are evaluated with incomplete physical modeling, which limits the physical validity of the resulting grasps. In this paper, we present StableGrasp, a differentiable simulation-based optimization framework that explicitly separates the visual hand pose from a control target that determines the grasping forces. Our method jointly optimizes hand geometry and control by minimizing the kinetic energy of the grasp in a differentiable simulator, while regularizing the hand geometry to preserve visual consistency and geometric plausibility. The reconstructed grasps are substantially more stable under physical simulation with complete physical modeling, while remaining visually consistent with the input images and geometrically plausible. Experiments show that our approach produces far more stable grasps than alternative hand-control strategies, benefiting visual-only grasp reconstruction pipelines by turning their outputs into physically stable grasps.

replace SplatQ: Interaction-Aware Pruning of 3D Gaussian Splatting via Quadratic Selection

Authors: Hongbin Ye, Haixu Liu, Shenwei Kang, Wenxiu Wei, Tinglan Wang, Hongdong Zhu, Fan Tang, Qi Gao, Yin Ma, Kai Wen, Hai Wei, Nuo Qian, Wei-Shi Zheng, Shaobo Chen, Fa-Ting Hong, Jingke Meng

Abstract: Existing 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) pruning methods primarily rely on global Top-K selection based on independent importance scores, lacking regional budget control and ignoring joint deletion effects. Consequently, they cannot ensure balanced reconstruction quality across a scene. We propose SplatQ, a combinatorial pruning algorithm that coordinates regional budget allocation and joint Gaussian selection under a fixed budget. SplatQ partitions Gaussians by spatial proximity and appearance similarity into size-limited blocks, decomposing global selection into subproblems of a specific size. It then smooths importance-based block retention quotas to prevent excessive budget concentration. Finally, it constructs a quadratic approximation of joint deletion effects using the responses of representative pixel colors to changes in Gaussian opacity. Individual responses, pairwise interactions, and quota penalties are incorporated into a quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) objective, solved using simulated annealing (SA) or a coherent Ising machine (CIM) to jointly select Gaussians. Experiments on 5 benchmarks demonstrate that SplatQ outperforms state-of-the-art pruning methods in aggregate reconstruction quality, spatial quality balance, and rendering speed. Ablations confirm that budget smoothing improves local detail reconstruction, while interaction modeling improves Gaussian selection quality under a fixed budget.

replace Composable Driving Style for End-to-End Autonomous Driving

Authors: Guantian Zheng, Lidong Yu, Tianyu Gao, Jiashu Li, Jiaxing Chen, Wilfried Ariel Mulyawan, Chen Lv

Abstract: End-to-end driving policies can achieve strong task performance while offering limited control over their driving style. Driving style describes how a vehicle accelerates, brakes, and maintains distance. Policies trained without explicit preference conditioning lack a direct mechanism for adjusting these behaviors at inference. We propose YOUDrive, a vision--language--action planner for continuous and composable driving-style control. Each teacher's behavior is distilled into a task vector, a learned parameter update that induces a style direction in the planner's representation. These directions can be scaled and combined at inference without retraining the backbone. Conditioned on these directions, a flow-matching decoder models a multimodal trajectory distribution and generates kinematically feasible motion alternatives. We introduce the Style Alignment Score (SAS) to describe human-referenced kinematic behavior after safety and task gating. On NAVSIM navtest, a single coefficient monotonically controls assertiveness across six teacher-derived personas, while PDMS remains above 0.90 across the tested individual and blended configurations, reaching 0.954. Further analyses reveal systematic policy-specific kinematics after controlling for scene context, whereas the evaluated discrete style labels provide limited additional explanatory power. Code, checkpoints, and the metric will be released.

Authors: Chao-Yeh Chen, Tiffany Chen, Lin Duan, Tianyi Hu, Yu-Hsiang Huang, Umar Iqbal, Jiefeng Li, Edy Lim, Yanchen Liu, Yihan Pang, Hang Qiu, Jun Saito, Shao Su, Qingsi Wang, Xiufeng Xie

Abstract: We introduce EgoHand, a monocular 3D hand reconstruction model that achieves state-of-the-art performance on egocentric video. Central to our approach is the curation of a high-quality training corpus of 14 million images, combining synthetic data, in-the-wild video, and multi-camera motion capture to balance scene diversity and supervision quality. replace GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Jiale Wang, Chen Zhao, Haoyang Zhang, Yanhao Wu, Fei He, Yanhu Shan, Rui Wu, Wei Ke, Tong Zhang

Abstract: Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. Non-pixel-aligned methods address this limitation by predicting a fixed number of Gaussians. Without explicit ray and depth constraints, these methods yield misplaced Gaussian centers, causing rendering artifacts under large viewpoint changes. To address this issue, we introduce GeoGSplat, a geometry-grounded feed-forward model that explicitly incorporates geometric priors into the non-pixel-aligned framework. A dual-branch encoder constructs Anchors and Triplane to capture coarse 3D structure and fine-grained scene features, respectively. Subsequently, a hierarchical decoder progressively decodes dense 3D points and Gaussian primitives, enabling 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GeoGSplat surpasses both state-of-the-art pixel-aligned and non-pixel-aligned Gaussian Splatting methods on DTU and NRGBD in terms of novel view synthesis and reconstruction. The advantage of our geometry-grounded paradigm further increases as target viewpoints move farther from the input cameras, demonstrating stronger generalization to large viewpoint changes. replace An Empirical Study on Learning Rooftop Solar Potential from Satellite Imagery Directly Without Explicit Geometry

Authors: Joshua Lee

Abstract: Rooftop solar potential estimation often relies on LiDAR, digital surface models (DSMs), or pipelines that explicitly reconstruct roof geometry before estimating solar potential. We propose Direct Rooftop Solar Prediction (DRSP), an end-to-end formulation that maps satellite imagery and coarse regional sunlight information directly to application-level solar outputs. DRSP neither requires explicit geometric representations as input nor predicts roof orientation, slope, height, DSMs, or other geometric quantities as intermediate outputs, and therefore requires no geometric intermediate supervision. To enable large-scale study of this formulation, we develop a programmatic data-curation pipeline that converts per-panel information from the Google Solar API into usable-rooftop masks and spatial solar-energy targets, yielding two geographically distinct datasets containing approximately 60K rooftops from the U.S. Midwest and 87K from Texas. We demonstrate the feasibility of DRSP and systematically characterize the effects of geographic training distribution, model depth, image resolution, regional sunlight conditioning, and heatmap regression objectives. Overall, our results suggest that useful rooftop-scale solar information can be learned directly from 2D imagery without explicit geometric intermediate representations, offering a simpler path toward large-scale solar assessment. Our code and dataset are available at https://github.com/joshualee124/direct_rooftop_solar_prediction.

replace Queryable Gaussian View Index: Text Retrieval and Selective Camera Localization

Authors: Tianyu Xiong, Rui Li, Jiaqi Yang

Abstract: We present Queryable Gaussian View Index (QGVI) for querying existing Gaussian scene assets and their associated calibrated reference data. Text-view retrieval and selective RGB camera localization return poses in the asset's coordinate frame. QGVI-T turns Gaussian-rendered views into a language-searchable index of scene locations, binding structured captions to known camera poses and place identifiers. Semantic and lexical retrieval select views from distinct places without online rendering, VLM calls, or a scene-specific pose network. The image route, QGVI-I, filters LoGS-based geometric estimates using distinct inlier query keypoints. It omits photometric refinement and preserves accepted pose coordinates. On nine development scenes, QGVI-T achieves 58.3% Top-1 and 78.3% Top-5 joint relevance-and-validity success, compared with 26.1% and 34.6% for our CityLoC reimplementation with refinement. QGVI-I achieves 97.3% scene-mean pose success at 1 m/2 degrees, with pooled false-acceptance and false-rejection rates of 0.707% and 1.142%. A paired cached evaluation retains all 1,775 initializer poses correct at this threshold. Recorded median text query latency is 0.020 s. Matched controls show competitive BM25 retrieval and slightly lower false acceptance for thresholded full LoGS; grid augmentation suggests modest gains on novel-view-derived descriptions. We report model-judged semantic success separately from source-camera recovery, with source-verified or estimated scales for geometric evaluation.

replace VideoEditor-MoE: Scaling Complex Video Editing with Mixture of Experts

Authors: Xiao Cao, Fuchen Long, Yansong Qu, Wen Xiao, Xin Zhang, Xinlong Sun, Xi Chen, Zhiyong Huang

Abstract: Complex video editing requires understanding complex instructions while jointly fulfilling multiple editing requests. Meeting these demands through dense model scaling substantially increases computational cost. We introduce VideoEditor-MoE, the first open-source mixture-of-experts framework for video editing. Beyond sparse MLP-MoE layers, MoE-Attention expands attention capacity through routed key--value experts while preserving global interactions among video tokens. Initialized from a 5B video editing model, VideoEditor-MoE scales to 14B and 56B parameters, with approximately 8B parameters activated per token in both variants. Comparisons with existing methods and two complementary baselines, Explicit-MoE and MLP-MoE, assess its benefits over explicit expert guidance and MLP-only expert architectures. Further analyses demonstrate that all experts remain active and reveal that learned routing responds more strongly to sample content and diffusion noise levels than to human-defined editing categories. Spatial analysis further indicates sensitivity to object-level semantic features. We also identify decoupled weight decay as a strategy for stabilizing expert scaling.

replace ReactWorld: Structuring Interaction Responses for Embodied Video Generation

Authors: Hanwei Liu, Huijia Zhang, Hanzhang Wang, Guangyu Liu, Hanbei Zhan, Linjie Hu, Bin Xiao, Xuan Zhou, Dong Zhang

Abstract: Embodied video world models predict scene changes caused by robot actions, yet generated object responses may remain inconsistent with task intent and contact dynamics. Critical responses in embodied scenes often concentrate in local regions and specific time intervals. Inspired by chain-of-thought reasoning, we introduce ReactWorld, an embodied video generation model guided by structured interaction anticipation. In the generation backbone's feature space, an interaction response field predictor anticipates how the interaction will unfold, explicitly describing participant roles, future motion response intensity, and local motion changes. A response feedback adapter forms increments to object features from the difference between the object branch's outputs with predicted and zeroed local dynamics inputs. It also converts predicted displacements into positional biases for temporal attention. These two mechanisms guide object feature updates and information retrieval across frames, respectively. Through interaction anticipation and generative feedback, ReactWorld achieves an average score of 77.51 on RBench, outperforming PhysisForcing by 3.29 points with improvements in all nine categories. On EZS-Bench, it achieves a Domain score of 86.98 and an average score of 81.87.

replace From Reflection to Co-Evolution: Self-Evolving Agents with Locally Verified Memory

Authors: Xinxiang Yin, Xinglang Zhang, Haodong Chen, Zhiqing Cui, Weidong Tang, Yuxuan Wang, Junrui Wang, Jingyi Zhang, Jianfeng Zhang, Jian Qian

Abstract: LLM agents have made impressive progress in coding and mathematical reasoning, motivating growing interest in learning from long-horizon interaction. Memory supports this process, but plausible lessons can be redundant or reinforce inappropriate actions. Can a memory's source decision provide a practical test for whether to retain it? We localize consequential trajectory steps, extract candidate memories, and replay each source decision with and without its candidate under the same context. This reveals two properties: most candidates barely shift the probed distribution, and both successful and failed source actions can be reinforced or suppressed. These observations motivate Anchor, which tests shift magnitude and source-role alignment before retention. Admitted memories guide later trajectories, enabling experience-driven improvement under a frozen policy. On held-out ALFWorld and WebShop tasks, Anchor improves success rate and mean score by up to 19.9% and 17.2%, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline for each backbone. With Qwen3.5-27B, it retains 41% fewer ALFWorld cards than unfiltered retention and outperforms all four WebShop cost-comparison baselines while using fewer tokens.

replace From Game Generation to Game Playing: Fully Automated Ability Training for Vision-Language-Action Models

Abstract: Training vision-language-action (VLA) models to follow gameplay instructions across diverse games is limited by the cost of collecting demonstrations. We introduce a framework that automatically generates game environments and expert demonstrations. An LLM-powered agent uses game-building tools to construct executable environments and tasks targeting specified skills. Programmatic experts then use privileged simulator state to generate aligned instructions, rendered observations, and keyboard and mouse actions. We train our game policy, Script2Play, by post-training a 4B vision-language model with behavior cloning on 150.3 hours of synthetic gameplay in Minecraft and Roblox, without human gameplay demonstrations or reinforcement learning. replace How to "train" a surgeon? Benchmarking generalist agents in surgical scene understanding

Authors: Gengyuan Zhang, Xiao Han, Shijie Zhou, Abdullah Koukash, Jingpei Wu, Jindong Gu, Yuqicheng Zhu, Nassir Navab, Volker Tresp

Abstract: Evaluating general-purpose multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for surgical scene understanding remains limited due to scarce surgical data and fragmented evaluation protocols. We address these challenges through a two-stage evaluation framework for surgical scene understanding. In Stage I, we introduce SurgKnowBench, a diagnostic benchmark that harmonizes public surgical datasets across procedures and tasks and evaluates proprietary and open-source MLLMs to map the boundary of pretrained surgical competence. SurgKnowBench reveals that current models retain partial visual priors but remain weak on relational and procedural understanding and workflow assessment. Building on this failure frontier and motivated by the scaffolded nature of surgical training, we introduce SurgAgentGym, an agentic environment where general-purpose MLLM agents can use bounded knowledge search over SurgWiki and general-purpose visual grounding for surgical tasks. SurgAgentGym evaluates whether generalist agents can improve surgical understanding at inference time through tool-mediated evidence use, without additional surgical training. Our results show that agentic inference narrows the gap mainly for perception-heavy tasks, while fine-grained localization and expert-style assessment remain persistent bottlenecks. replace 2D Gum-Net: Unsupervised Dense Elastic Image Registration via Symmetric Correlation and Spectral Pooling

Authors: Aarya Bhave, Anushka Ajay Bhave, Akanksha Narula, Marius Sebastian Shokoui, Alina Hafner

Abstract: Dense non-rigid registration, the recovery of a pixel-wise deformation field that warps one image into geometric agreement with another, is fundamental across medical imaging, structural biology, and correspondence estimation, yet learning-based approaches face a severe bottleneck: ground-truth deformation fields are notoriously difficult to obtain for real data. We present 2D Gum-Net, an end-to-end architecture that predicts dense elastic deformation fields fully unsupervised. We transfer the Geometric Unsupervised Matching Network (Gum-Net), originally developed for 3D subtomogram alignment in cryo-electron tomography, to 2D elastic registration: all three modules are redesigned in 2D, and the rigid transformation head is replaced by a Dense Spatial Transformer that regresses a sparse control-point grid, lifted to a full-resolution field $\Phi$ by bicubic interpolation. Downsampling uses DCT spectral pooling, implemented as frozen-basis matrix multiplications ($\approx 50\times$ GPU speedup), preserving the fine, quasi-periodic structure that max and average pooling destroy. We evaluate on contact-based fingerprints, a challenging testbed for this task: locally self-similar ridge patterns make correspondence maximally ambiguous, skin pressed against a sensor generates authentic large-displacement elastic distortion, and minutiae act as precise landmarks through which an independent matcher externally verifies the topological correctness of predicted fields. Trained purely on synthetic impressions and evaluated zero-shot on real benchmarks, our method improves structural correlation by up to 15.7% on FVC2004 and raises genuine match scores under an independent matcher while leaving imposter scores essentially unchanged. This suggests that the predicted deformations restore true structure without fabricating correspondences.

replace DailyWildfireCAN: A Dataset and Benchmark for Next-Day Wildfire Spread Prediction in Canada

Authors: Mahacine Ettahri, Hager Radi Abdelwahed, Toluwaleke Olutayo, Daniel Aloise, Ga\'etan Marceau Caron

Abstract: Wildfires represent a critical ecological and socio-economic challenge in Canada, where vast boreal forests and a warming climate have amplified the frequency and severity of extreme fire seasons. Predicting next-day wildfire spread can support fire management, but requires capturing complex interactions among weather, fuels, and terrain. Deep learning has emerged as a promising approach to learning these relationships from heterogeneous environmental data, yet its development and evaluation depend on representative datasets. Existing deep learning research on wildfire spread relies predominantly on US-focused datasets, leaving Canadian fire environments underrepresented. To address this gap, we present DailyWildfireCAN, a multi-modal, multi-temporal dataset and benchmark for predicting next-day wildfire spread across Canada. The dataset tracks continuous fire progression over multi-day events at a high spatial and temporal resolution of 90-m and 24-hour, respectively. This resolution preserves fine-scale information relevant to fire-edge geometry, local fuel variations, and terrain effects, supporting research toward improved daily wildfire spread forecasting. DailyWildfireCAN covers 2,440 wildfire events across eight fire seasons 2017-2024 and integrates 33 features across diverse modalities, including topography, meteorology, land cover, vegetation and forest structure, human influence indices, and satellite imagery. We establish a comprehensive benchmark comparing mono-temporal and multi-temporal prediction models, accompanied by a multi-dimensional evaluation framework assessing robustness across wildfire characteristics, geographic regions, and temporal conditions, spatial prediction quality, and sensitivity to reference label variations.

replace WorldDraft: If Code Builds the World, Can Video Models Just Render It?

Authors: Zhiyu Hou, Wen-Jie Shu, Harold Haodong Chen, Weilin Ruan, Chenfei Liao, Liuzhou Zhang, Disen Lan, Xiaogang Xu, Qi Chen, Ser-Nam Lim

Abstract: Multimodal coding agents decouple 4D world construction from visual generation, repurposing video models from world generators toward generative renderers. This division of labor raises a fundamental question: if code builds the world, can video models just render it? A reliable generative renderer hinges on two complementary criteria: (i) world preservation, retaining prescribed 4D kinematics and camera paths; and (ii) visual realization, persistently binding text-specified target attributes over time. To evaluate this capability, we introduce WorldDraft, a benchmark of $432$ strictly paired test cases across $48$ executable 4D world families spanning six dynamic challenge categories. Under a $3 \times 3$ factorial design, WorldDraft orthogonally crosses draft granularity (abstract proxy to previs) and appearance alignment (neutral to conflicting) under identical dynamics. Extensive evaluations across $12$ representative models reveal that: (I) world preservation and visual realization remain in stark operational tension; (II) richer draft scaffolding anchors structure but paradoxically impairs appearance fidelity; and (III) conflicting source cues trigger severe source leakage (up to $64\%$), exposing fragile cross-modal disentanglement. To mitigate these bottlenecks, we propose WorldDraft-Primer, a plug-and-play visual prompting strategy that consistently improves both objectives across frozen video renderers. Together, WorldDraft and WorldDraft-Primer lay a foundation for bridging programmatic 4D simulation with video generation, advancing physically grounded generative rendering.

replace Selective Generative Restoration for Video Deraining via Uncertainty-Aware Diffusion

Authors: Yi'ang Chen, Han Li, Laibin Chang, Hui Liu, Kui Jiang, Junjun Jiang

Abstract: Video deraining is challenging due to rain streaks, occlusions, and atmospheric effects. Existing methods mainly follow a deterministic restoration paradigm, assuming that clean scenes can be uniquely recovered from rainy observations. However, rain degradation is spatially heterogeneous. Reliable regions require faithful reconstruction, whereas severely occluded regions contain ambiguous content that require stronger prior-driven inference. Therefore, conventional deterministic restoration often produces over-smoothed results, while global generative restoration may introduce unnecessary hallucinations. We introduce a Selective Generative Restoration paradigm for video deraining, where generative priors are adaptively activated according to restoration uncertainty. Based on this principle, we propose Selective Generative Video Deraining (SGVD), an uncertainty-aware diffusion framework that enables adaptive fidelity preservation and generative recovery. Specifically, SGVD exploits stochastic diversity between dual one-step diffusion predictions to estimate region-level restoration uncertainty, which determines where observation fidelity should be preserved and where generative inference should be activated. Furthermore, an anchor-guided trajectory consistency mechanism is proposed to regularize stochastic diffusion evolution across frames, reducing temporal generation drift and improving video coherence. To achieve practical efficiency, SGVD adopts one-step diffusion inference while maintaining uncertainty-aware generative capability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SGVD achieves superior restoration performance, perceptual quality, and temporal consistency compared with state-of-the-art video deraining methods.

replace RICapBench: A Fine-Grained Visual Understanding Benchmark Dataset for Vision-Language Models in Remote Sensing

Authors: Xue Wang, Yubin Xu, Kun Tan

Abstract: Advances in vision-language models (VLMs) have improved remote sensing image analysis and supported geospatial decision-making. However, fine-grained understanding of remote sensing imagery remains challenging for VLMs. Large variations in object scale, complex geospatial relations among instances, and limited visual detail hinder precise instance localization, visible attribute identification, and spatial relation understanding, potentially leading to descriptions that lack visual grounding. Existing datasets and benchmarks insufficiently assess end-to-end instance localization and captioning without target-specific textual cues. To support this evaluation, we introduce RICapBench, a dataset and benchmark that unifies instance localization and fine-grained captioning in remote sensing. RICapBench contains 245,466 instance-level annotations and 498,005 spatial relation tuples spanning general remote sensing, UAV remote sensing, and dense small-object scenarios. Each annotation pairs an object's oriented bounding box with a natural language description of its visible attributes and spatial relations to surrounding instances and environmental elements. To evaluate the accuracy and completeness of spatial relation captioning, we propose a remote sensing spatial relation parser and the RelCap-F1 metric. We combine RelCap-F1 with instance localization and attribute captioning metrics into a composite metric, RICScore. Our evaluation of 15 advanced open-source and closed-source VLMs identifies instance localization as a major bottleneck for these models. LocateAnything achieves the highest RICScore of 31.68, but scores lower on spatial relation captioning than other task-fine-tuned open-source VLMs. Code and dataset are available at https://github.com/Xldln/RICapBench.

URLs: https://github.com/Xldln/RICapBench.

replace Learning the Missing Derivative: Neural Multiple-Scattering Operators for Inverse Volume Rendering

Authors: Sipeng Chen, Shibo Li

Abstract: Reconstructing optically thick media from photographs requires modeling multiple scattering and its response to changes in extinction. Frozen-source tomography discards this derivative; differentiable path tracing estimates it through expensive, noisy sampling. We instead predict the multiple-scattering field with a neural operator and differentiate through it during inverse rendering, while computing transmittance and single scattering directly. A further challenge arises during optimization: an operator that predicts the field accurately on held-out media can still produce poor derivatives at the density states visited by the optimizer. We address this by fine-tuning on states generated during inversions driven by the operator, using Monte Carlo scattering-field supervision. On held-out optically thick clouds, our method improves relighting over frozen-source tomography at equal time and outperforms differentiable path tracing given substantially more time. Training on inversion trajectories also improves derivative alignment at visited states and reduces optimization drift.

replace Perceptual Weight Space Programming for Diffusion Super-Resolution

Authors: Chenzhi Zheng, Zhuoran Zheng

Abstract: Diffusion models for image super-resolution can recover scene content while producing distracting artifacts or unbalanced colors. We study whether a large language model (LLM) can improve perceptual quality by editing a small subset of existing U-Net weights without a clean reference image. The proposed framework provides the editor with architecture code, selected weights, activation summaries, and sampling previews. It permits up to 1,020 candidate trials while limiting accepted changes to three layers and fewer than 0.1\% of model parameters. The evaluation protocol uses ResShift, SinSR, and SUPIR and compares GPT-6 Astra with a specified Qwen3-VL model under matched access. Feedback metrics guide editing, while separate quality metrics and blinded human comparisons evaluate the final weights. We also report an executed color calibration experiment using real photographs and two small residual networks. This experiment demonstrates correction of a fixed color shift and reveals that an improving feedback score can accompany worse performance on unseen scenes. A separate lightweight super-resolution experiment isolates modest changes in luminance detail while preserving color. The findings motivate restricted updates and independent evaluation of perceptual weight editing.

replace SCALE: Towards Whole-Slide Counterfactual Explanations in Cancer Histopathology

Authors: Weiyi Qin, Yingci Liu-Swetz, Shiwei Tan, Yi Wang, Mohammad Alexanderani, Dimitrios Korentzelos, Peizi Li, Soo Yeon Sohn, Xintong Wang, Hao Peng, Yue Guo, Yuzhe Yang, Han Zhao, Hao Wang

Abstract: Accurate cancer diagnosis depends on histopathological evaluation, in which tumor morphology drives disease classification and therapeutic decision-making. While deep learning models have demonstrated strong performance in predicting diagnostic and prognostic properties from histopathology images, their clinical adoption remains limited by a lack of interpretability. Unlike conventional medical images where diagnostic findings are spatially localized, histopathological images are characterized by complex textural patterns distributed across the entire whole slide image (WSI), making it difficult to explain model predictions through region-based localization alone. Counterfactual explanations offer a promising alternative by asking "what changes in the image could have altered the model prediction." To achieve this, we propose SCALE, a framework for generating such counterfactual explanations for histopathological DL models. Across nine counterfactual directions spanning multiple cancer types, SCALE generates counterfactual explanations that consistently shift model predictions toward the intended diagnostic target while maintaining biological feasibility. In a blinded review by pathologists, SCALE demonstrated superior morphological plausibility compared to baseline methods (win rate of 91.7%) and robust counterfactual effectiveness (target consistency of 96.7%). These results suggest that SCALE generates morphologically plausible and effective counterfactual explanations that can be applied to improve the interpretability of deep learning models for histopathology.

replace GameWM: Toward Next-Generation Video Games with Interaction-Centric World Models

Authors: Bing He, Runze Wang, Fei Zha, Zhongyuan Wu, Ke Xu, Tao Zhuang, Yuxuan Xie, Zhongyuan Liu

Abstract: For more than half a century, video games have pursued better graphics, more natural interaction, and more engaging gameplay. Generative video models (VMs) now bear on all three. Neural networks already draw most of the pixels in shipped games, and recent world models let a lightweight game engine keep the world state while a VM turns the engine's coarse proxy video into final frames. These systems, however, treat the VM as a renderer that only restyles motion the engine has already authored, which leaves most of its generative capacity unused and limits the diversity a game can offer. We present GameWM, a design for next-generation games built on a clear division of labor. The GameWM-engine keeps what must be exact: rules, state, camera, and the layout of the world, which also spares the video model from having to remember it. The GameWM-VM generates what is expensive to author by hand: the look of the world and the detail of the player's interactions. One game mechanic can then be realized in many visually and physically distinct ways, while its outcome stays exactly as the engine computed it. To realize this division of labor, we build GameWM as a complete system: a data engine centered on interactions rather than exploration, an interface through which authored character sheets, scene art, and skill descriptions enter the model, and a model family from 5B to 33B parameters with a real-time causal variant for play. Together they give every player a fresh version of the same game: each player's own character, world, and skills turn the same engine mechanics into a new experience, while every event keeps the timing and outcome the GameWM-engine computed.

replace HIVE-ECIA: Identity-Aware Historical Evidence Acquisition for Bandwidth-Efficient Multi-Object Tracking

Authors: Hai Nguyen, Tarik Taleb, Qize Guo

Abstract: Panoramic multi-object tracking (MOT) requires reliable identity association across a 360{\deg} field of view, yet bandwidth-constrained operation forces a trade-off between low-resolution (LR) identity ambiguity and costly high-resolution (HR) transmission. We propose HIVE-ECIA, an identity-aware framework that selectively retrieves historical HR evidence to recover fragmented identities without modifying detection boxes or the underlying tracker. An ECIA Base-Guided query policy determines when additional HR evidence is warranted; HIVE ranks historical LR observations to select an informative retrieval timestamp; and ECIA Full integrates the retrieved HR crop through learned gated fusion and conservative identity safeguards. We further introduce DualPano-MOT, a real-world dual-fisheye panoramic evaluation dataset featuring controlled pedestrian interactions, panorama-seam challenges, and occlusions, with model-assisted, manually verified tracking annotations. On held-out QuadTrack sequences and zero-shot DualPano-MOT, HIVE-ECIA improves identity association across four MOT trackers without tracker-specific retraining. Across these trackers, identity switches decrease by up to 21.8% and 23.4%, while the largest IDF1 gains reach 3.81 and 6.74 percentage points, respectively. Under an offline JPEG-based acquisition model, our framework reduces encoded data volume by 89.2% and 79.3% relative to continuous Full-HR acquisition.

replace What Matters for Cross-Modal Alignment

Authors: Yipeng Sun, Jiajun Wang, Siyuan Mei, Yan Xia, Siming Bayer, Vincent Christlein, Andreas Maier

Abstract: The Platonic Representation Hypothesis posits that models trained on different modalities converge toward a shared representation. Related work aligns frozen image and text encoders on natural images without paired data. We test zero-pair alignment on five datasets across four modalities: natural images, environmental audio, chest radiographs, and 3D computed tomography (CT). It succeeds only on COCO and fails on the other four. We analyze the failures and find that alignment must first locate the subspace where the modalities co-vary and then orient the mapping within it. Consistent with previous studies, medical imaging is the hardest case: its shared subspace carries the smallest fraction of the variance. When this fraction is small, alignment needs image-text pairs, and random-matrix theory predicts how many pairs are needed. These findings motivate LightAlign: a few pairs let a ridge regression between two frozen encoders locate and orient the mapping simultaneously, and a classifier fitted only on report text then predicts findings from the mapped images. On 3D CT, LightAlign surpasses models trained end to end on image-report pairs without finding-level labels: with only 200 pairs, LightAlign outperforms CT-CLIP on CT-RATE (0.754 vs. 0.733 AUROC), and with only 100 CT-RATE pairs, it does so on RAD-ChestCT, an out-of-distribution dataset from a different hospital. On CheXpert, it reaches 0.862 mean AUROC with only about 5% as many pairs as models trained on MIMIC-CXR.

Abstract: This article deals with the problem of detecting the position of power lines in airborne laser scanning point clouds. We address both the problem of segmenting power lines from background like ground and vegetation and of identifying individual lines. For segmentation we compare a rule-based approach with one based on transfer learning (Point-SAM). For individual line detection, we derive the mathematical formulas for RANSAC applied to 3D catenaries and compare it with a non-parametric method (TriplClust). All methods are evaluated on a newly created dataset "PowLiDFR", which we make publicly available. replace Event-Triggered Visual Perception for Real-Time Harvest Outcome Estimation in Mechanical Blueberry Harvesting

Authors: Nicolas Miroslav Hus\'ar, Jelle Driessen, Petra Bosilj, Ba\c{s}ak Sak\c{c}ak

Abstract: Mechanical blueberry harvesting requires a balance between removing ripe fruit and avoiding premature removal of unripe berries that could remain on the bush for a later harvest. However, operators generally lack real-time information about the maturity composition of the fruit being collected. This study develops an event-triggered machine-vision system that measures the maturity of harvested blueberries directly on the harvester. When a filled crate is moved by the machine, an RGB image is automatically captured and analysed using a three-class detector that identifies ripe, semi-ripe, and unripe berries. The system operates locally on the existing CPU-only industrial computer and therefore requires neither continuous image processing nor dedicated GPU hardware. During field testing, 79 crate images containing 48,634 visible blueberries in manually verified reference annotations were evaluated. The vision system estimated an overall ripe-fruit fraction of 90.22%, compared with 90.97% determined manually. Per-image ripe-fruit estimates showed a mean absolute error of 0.935 percentage points and a Pearson correlation of r=0.967. Maturity estimates remained similar between the two sides of the harvester for which the light conditions varied despite poorer absolute counting performance under direct sunlight. All field events were processed within the available 14 s per-side crate interval. The proposed approach provides a practical means of quantifying one component of harvest selectivity while the machine is operating. Such information could support operator decision-making and, when combined with measurements of fruit recovery and losses, contribute to future feedback-based control of mechanical harvesting.

replace SRED: Sparse Regional Evidence Distillation Across Magnifications in Whole-Slide Imaging

Authors: Zhiyuan Yang, Jiahao Cheng, Vincent Quoc-Huy Trinh, Mahdi S. Hosseini

Abstract: In computational pathology, high-magnification whole-slide image analysis preserves tissue detail but requires encoding many patches, whereas lower magnification reduces computation by covering a higher field of view per patch while sacrificing detail. Existing cross-magnification distillation matches features that often encode detail unreachable by low-magnification input, sometimes requiring slide-level labels that limit scalability. To address those issues, we introduce Sparse Regional Evidence Distillation (SRED), which transfers regional event occurrences that encode reachable high-level interactions between details without slide-level labels. We use a sparse autoencoder (SAE) to learn an event dictionary from 20x teacher features. Logical OR pooling records whether each event is detected in any spatially aligned patch, retaining both localized and shared evidence within the corresponding 5x region. A pretrained 5x encoder is fine-tuned to predict these regional targets without slide-level labels. Visual analysis identifies dictionary components responsive to recognizable histological patterns, supporting target interpretation. Compared with native 5x features, SRED improves average macro-F1 by 1.1 points across seven classification and grading cohorts and average C-index by 1.7 points across five survival cohorts. Runtime matches native 5x, with an average 11.58x speedup over 20x.

replace SIDUMN: Self-Supervised Image Denoising Under Symmetric Multiplicative Noise

Authors: Nyan Htet Naing, Gyeongha Hwang

Abstract: Self-supervised denoising is challenging when the corruption is multiplicative and signal-dependent. In this letter, we propose SIDUMN, a symmetric noisier framework for self-supervised denoising under mean-one symmetric multiplicative noise. Given noisy observations and the noise law, the method samples an independent synthetic noise factor from the same law and constructs two complementary noisier images by applying the factor and its reflection about one. The denoising network is trained to predict one noisier image from the other, and at inference the reconstructions of $K$ such antithetic pairs are averaged. We show that the population target of the training loss is the clean conditional mean minus a multiplicative cross-term. We further prove that, for any trained network, antithetic averaging never increases the expected squared error of a single noisier-input reconstruction. Numerical results validate the theoretical findings and the proposed method.

replace Classification of Tissues on MALDI-MSI Image Modality for Pancreatic Cancer Studies

Authors: Abubakar Qahir, Jenny Benois-Pineau, Victoria Bourgeais, Fabien Baldacci, Sandrine Dabernat, Nicolas Desbenoit, M\'elanie Moreau, Mehdi Boubaddi, Marion Marty

Abstract: Mass Spectrometry Imaging (MSI) spatially maps molecular distributions in tissue sections. Despite the availability of preprocessing tools for MSI data, building end-to-end workflows suitable for deep learning remains challenging. The proposed pipeline computational time is of 20 min in average compared to a full working day of the reference interactive workflow.
